228.94945pt\raise-58.24501pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 266.10225pt\raise-58.24501pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{L}^{\mathsf{f}}_{2r+1}\otimes\mathcal{Z}^{\mathsf{f}}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

(52)

where similar to the previous

\displaystyle\pi_{2r+1}:\bigoplus_{w\geq 1}\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{f}}_{w}

\displaystyle\to\mathcal{L}^{\mathsf{f}}_{2r+1},

\displaystyle\overline{\mathsf{a}\circ\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}}\colon\mathcal{L}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\mathcal{L}^{\mathsf{f}},

are the canonical projections.

Observe as in Lemma
2.42
, the maps
D_{2r+1}:\mathcal{Z}^{\mathsf{f}}\to\mathcal{L}^{\mathsf{f}}_{2r+1}\otimes\mathcal{Z}^{\mathsf{f}}
in the lower line are again derivations.

Proposition 5.50
.

Assume the free odd generation conjecture
5.44
for
\mathfrak{dm}_{0}
. For each
r\in\mathbb{N}
the element

\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}_{2r+1}=\pi_{2r+1}(\mathsf{a}(\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(2r+1)))\in\mathcal{L}^{\mathsf{f}}_{2r+1}

is non-zero.

Proof.

This claim is just a reformulation of Corollary
5.48
.
∎

Lemma 5.51
.

Let
r,n,k\geq 1
be natural numbers, then we have

D_{2n+1}(\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(2r+1)\cdot\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(2)^{k})=\delta_{r,n}\cdot\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}_{2r+1}\otimes\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(2)^{k}.

(53)

where
\delta_{r,n}
is the Kronecker delta.

Proof.

Recall
\Delta_{\operatorname{Gon}}(\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(2)^{k})=\mathbf{1}\otimes\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(2)^{k}
and

\displaystyle\Delta_{\operatorname{Gon}}(\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(2r+1))=\mathsf{a}(\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(2r+1))\otimes\mathbf{1}+\mathbf{1}\otimes\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(2r+1).

We then compute

\displaystyle\Delta_{\operatorname{Gon}}^{\prime}(\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(2r+1)\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(2)^{k})

\displaystyle=\Delta_{\operatorname{Gon}}(\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(2r+1)\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(2)^{k})-\mathbf{1}\otimes\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(2r+1)\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(2)^{k}

\displaystyle=\Delta_{\operatorname{Gon}}(\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(2r+1))\Delta_{\operatorname{Gon}}(\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(2)^{k})-\mathbf{1}\otimes\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(2r+1)\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(2)^{k}

\displaystyle=\Big(\mathsf{a}(\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(2r+1))\otimes\mathbf{1}+\mathbf{1}\otimes\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(2r+1)\Big)\Big(\mathbf{1}\otimes\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(2)^{k}\Big)-\mathbf{1}\otimes\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(2r+1)\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(2)^{k}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{a}(\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(2r+1))\otimes\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(2)^{k},

which in turn yields

\displaystyle D_{2n+1}

\displaystyle(\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(2r+1)\zeta(2)^{k})=\pi_{2n+1}(\mathsf{a}(\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(2r+1)))\otimes\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(2)^{k}.\qed

Conjecture 5.52

(Kernel conjecture)
.

Define

D_{<N}=\bigoplus_{3\leq 2r+1<N}D_{2r+1},

(54)

then we have
\ker(D_{<N})\cap\mathcal{Z}^{\mathsf{f}}_{N}=\mathbb{Q}\,\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(N)
for all
N\geq 2
.

Remark 5.53
.

Observe that
\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(N)\in\ker(D_{<N})
for all
N\geq 2
. This follows from Proposition
5.20
for even
N
and from Lemma
5.43
together with Corollary
5.48
for odd
N
.

Theorem 5.54
.

The free odd generation conjecture
5.44
for
\mathfrak{dm}_{0}
implies the Kernel conjecture
5.52
, i.e., we have for all
N\geq 2

\ker(D_{<N})\cap\mathcal{Z}^{\mathsf{f}}_{N}=\mathbb{Q}\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(N).

Proof.

Recall that
\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(N)\in\ker(D_{<N})
.
For the other inclusion we employ the isomorphism of algebras with coaction

\Phi\colon(\mathcal{Z}^{\mathsf{f}},\cdot,\Delta_{\operatorname{Gon}})\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}(\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{f}},\shuffle,\Delta_{\text{dec}})

from Proposition
5.47
. In particular this yields for all
N\in\mathbb{N}

a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathcal{Z}^{\mathsf{f}}_{N}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{D_{<N}}
\scriptstyle{\Phi}
\textstyle{\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{f}}_{N}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{D_{<N}}
\textstyle{\bigoplus\limits_{1<2r+1<N}\!\!\!\mathcal{L}^{\mathsf{f}}_{2r+1}\otimes\mathcal{Z}^{\mathsf{f}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\overline{\Phi}\otimes\Phi}
\textstyle{\bigoplus\limits_{1<2r+1<N}\!\!\!L_{2r+1}\otimes\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{f}}}

(55)

Hence, if
\xi\in\ker(D_{<N})\cap\mathcal{Z}^{\mathsf{f}}_{N}
, then (
55
) implies
\Phi(\xi)\in\ker(D_{<N})
. So by the Proposition
2.48
, we obtain that
\Phi(\xi)=\alpha\,s_{N}
for some
\alpha\in\mathbb{Q}
. Hence
\xi=\alpha\,\Phi^{-1}(s_{N})=\alpha\,\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(N)
.
∎

Example 5.55
.

The direct sum in (
54
) is empty for
N=3
, hence
D_{<3}\equiv 0
is the zero map.
Conjecture
5.52
would thus imply that
\mathcal{Z}^{\mathsf{f}}_{3}=\mathbb{Q}\,\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(3)
, so in particular
\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(2,1)
would be a rational multiple of
\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(3)

(possibly zero). In fact, in Example
5.15
we saw that Euler’s identity
\zeta(3)=\zeta(2,1)
follows from the
extended double shuffle relations, hence
\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(3)=\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(2,1)
.

Example 5.56
.

We give here some potential applications of the Kernel conjecture. Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
be a positive integer.

1.

We compute
D_{<2n}(\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(\{2\}^{n}))
. Let
r\in\mathbb{N}
with
2r+1<2n
. Each term in
D_{2r+1}(\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(\{2\}^{n}))
corresponds to a consecutive
subword of
(x_{0}x_{1})^{n}\in\mathcal{X}^{*}
of weight
2r+1
. Note that such a subword always starts and ends in the same letter for parity reasons.
This can be depicted on a semicircle as:
x_{1}
x_{0}
x_{1}
x_{1}
x_{1}
x_{1}
x_{1}
x_{1}
x_{0}
x_{0}
x_{0}
x_{0}
x_{0}
x_{0}
x_{0}
\dots

So we have
\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(\{2\}^{n})\in\ker(D_{2r+1})
for all
2r+1<2n
, hence
\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(\{2\}^{n})\in\ker(D_{<2n})
.
We therefore expect by Conjecture
5.52
that
\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(\{2\}^{n})\in\mathbb{Q}\,\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(2n)
. In fact, this holds by Proposition
5.20
.
A generalisation of this method can be found in
[
15
]
.

2.

We compute
D_{<n+2}(\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(2,\{1\}^{n}))
. Let
r\in\mathbb{N}
with
2r+1<n+2
. Since
\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(2,\{1\}^{n})=\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(x_{0}x_{1}^{n+1})
there are only two kind of strict subwords whose boundaries are not both
x_{1}
. They can be depicted as

Both corresponding terms vanish since
\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(x_{1}^{2r+1})=0
by (
41
). So we have
\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(2,\{1\}^{n})\in\ker(D_{2r+1})
for all
2r+1<n+2
, hence
\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(2,\{1\}^{n})\in\ker(D_{<n+2})
. By the Kernel conjecture
5.52
we expect that
\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(2,\{1\}^{n})\in\mathbb{Q}\,\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(n+2)
.
Using the evaluation map would imply

\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(2,\{1\}^{n})=\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(n+2).

as
\zeta(2,\{1\}^{n})=\zeta(n+2)
.
Remarkably, there are two independent ways to show that multiple zeta values satisfy this identity. At first it is an example of
duality relations
,
i. e.
\zeta(x_{0}^{k_{1}-1}x_{1}\cdots x_{0}^{k_{d}-1}x_{1})=\zeta(x_{0}x_{1}^{k_{d}-1}\cdots x_{0}x_{1}^{k_{1}-1})
for integers
k_{1}>1
and
k_{2},\dots,k_{d}\geq 1
.
Alternatively, as shown in
[
33
]
, this identity also follows from the extended double shuffle relations and therefore we also get a unconditional proof without refering to the Kernel conjecture in this case.

6
Brown’s theorem for formal multiple zeta values

In this section we are interested in formulas for the multiple zeta values with level zero and one (cf Definition
4.1
).
We already understand

H(n)=\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(\{2\}^{n})
, since by Proposition
5.20
we have explicit formulae for

\displaystyle\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(2k)\cdot\,\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(\{2\}^{n-k})\in\mathbb{Q}\cdot\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(2)^{n}.

Theorem 6.1

(Zagier, 2012)
.

Let
a,b\geq 0
. Then we have an identity of multiple zeta values

\zeta(\{2\}^{a},3,\{2\}^{b})=\sum_{r=1}^{a+b+1}c_{a,b}^{r}\,\zeta(2r+1)\zeta(\{2\}^{a+b+1-r})

with
c_{a,b}^{r}
from Definition
4.30
, i.e.

c_{a,b}^{r}=2\cdot(-1)^{r}\Bigg(\binom{2r}{2b+2}-(1-2^{-2r})\binom{2r}{2a+1}\Bigg).

Brown showed that this formula also holds for motivic MZVs. We will prove the analogue statement for formal MZVs, assuming the Kernel conjecture
5.52
.

6.1
Proof of Zagier’s formula

Zagier’s theorem is a vast refinement of Proposition
5.22
, i.e. of the identity

\displaystyle\sum_{a+b=n}\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(\{2\}^{a},3,\{2\}^{b})=\sum_{i=0}^{n}(-1)^{i}\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(2i+3)\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(\{2\}^{n-i}).

Unfortunately, by now only analytical proofs of his result are known. We follow in our presentation the paper

[
34
]
.

Theorem 6.2
.

Let
a,b\geq 0
be integers, then the following three quantities are equal

\displaystyle H(a,b)

\displaystyle=\zeta(\{2\}^{a},3,\{2\}^{b})

(56)

\displaystyle I(a,b)

\displaystyle=\frac{\pi^{2a}2^{2b+3}}{(2a+1)!(2b+2)!}\int\limits_{0}^{\pi/2}x^{2b+2}\Big(1-\frac{2x}{\pi}\Big)^{2a+1}\cot(x)dx

(57)

\displaystyle\hat{H}(a,b)

\displaystyle=\sum_{r=1}^{a+b+1}c_{a,b}^{r}\,\zeta(2r+1)\zeta(\{2\}^{a+b+1-r}).

(58)

Essential ingredients for the proof are the following three lemmas.

Lemma 6.3
.

For any polynomial
P(x)\in\mathbb{C}[x]
with
P(0)=P(1)=0
, we have

\displaystyle\int\limits_{0}^{1}P(x)\cot\big(\frac{\pi x}{2}\big)dx

\displaystyle=2\sum_{k=1}^{\lfloor\deg(P)/2\rfloor}(-1)^{k}\Big(\big(1-2^{-2k}\big)P^{(2k)}(1)+P^{(2k)}(0)\Big)\frac{\zeta(2k+1)}{\pi^{2k+1}},

(59)

where
P^{(2k)}(x)
denotes the
(2k)
-th derivative of
P(x)
.

Proof.

This is a special case of
[
34
, Lemma 2.3]
.
∎

Lemma 6.4
.

Let
r\in\mathbb{N}
, then we have

\displaystyle\frac{\arcsin(x)^{2r}}{(2r)!}=\frac{1}{4^{r}}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{4^{n}}{n^{2}\binom{2n}{n}}\,x^{2n}\sum\limits_{n_{1}<n_{2}<...<n_{r-1}<n}\frac{1}{n_{1}^{2}n_{2}^{2}...n_{r-1}^{2}}.

(60)

Proof.

For a proof we refer to
[
34
, Eq. (2)]
.
∎

Lemma 6.5
.

Let
n\geq 1
,
a\geq 0
, then we have

\displaystyle\int\limits_{0}^{1}x^{2n-1}\frac{(2\arccos(x))^{2a+1}}{(2a+1)!}dx=\frac{\binom{2n}{n}\pi}{2^{2n+1}n}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n<m_{1}<m_{2}<...<m_{a}}\frac{1}{m_{1}^{2}m_{2}^{2}...m_{a}^{2}},

(61)

where the sum is set to be
1
if
a=0
.

Proof.

For a proof we refer to
[
34
, Lemma 3.1]
.
∎

We are now prepared for the proof of Zagier’s theorem.

Proof of Theorem
6.2
.

At first we observe that via the change of variables
x\mapsto\dfrac{\pi x}{2}
we obtain

\displaystyle I(a,b)

\displaystyle=\frac{\pi^{2a}2^{2b+3}}{(2a+1)!(2b+2)!}\int\limits_{0}^{\pi/2}x^{2b+2}\Big(1-\frac{2x}{\pi}\Big)^{2a+1}\cot(x)dx

\displaystyle=\frac{\pi^{2a+2b+2}2}{(2a+1)!(2b+2)!}\int\limits_{0}^{1}x^{2b+2}\big(1-x\big)^{2a+1}\cot\big(\frac{\pi x}{2}\big)dx

Applying Lemma
6.3
with
P(x)=x^{2b+2}(1-x)^{2a+1}
yields

\displaystyle\int\limits_{0}^{1}x^{2b+2}\big(1-x\big)^{2a+1}\cot\big(\frac{\pi x}{2}\big)dx

\displaystyle=(2a+1)!(2b+2)!

\displaystyle\cdot 2\sum_{k=0}^{a+b+1}(-1)^{k}\Big[\binom{2k}{2b+2}-\big(1-2^{-2k}\big)\binom{2k}{2a+1}\Big]\frac{\zeta(2k+1)}{(2a+2b+3-2k)!\pi^{2k+1}}

Now using Proposition
5.20
the equality
I(a,b)=\hat{H}(a,b)
follows by rearranging the above equalities.

To obtain the second equality we start with the change of variables
x\mapsto\arcsin(x)
. Using in addition that
\arccos(x)=\pi/2-\arcsin(x)
we get

\displaystyle I(a,b)

\displaystyle=\frac{2^{2a+2b+4}}{(2a+1)!(2b+2)!\pi}\int\limits_{0}^{\pi/2}x^{2b+2}\Big(\frac{\pi}{2}-x\Big)^{2a+1}\cot(x)dx

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{\pi}\int\limits_{0}^{1}\frac{(2\arcsin(x))^{2b+2}}{(2b+2)!}\frac{(2\arccos(x))^{2a+1}}{(2a+1)!}\frac{1}{x}dx.

If we replace the first factor under the integral by its Taylor expansion given in Lemma

6.4
, then the resulting sum of integrals are exactly those of
6.5
, therefore we get

\displaystyle\int\limits_{0}^{1}\frac{(2\arcsin(x))^{2b+2}}{(2b+2)!}\frac{(2\arccos(x))^{2a+1}}{(2a+1)!}\frac{1}{x}dx

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{4^{n}}{n^{2}\binom{2n}{n}}\sum\limits_{n_{1}<n_{2}<...<n_{b}<n}\frac{1}{n_{1}^{2}n_{2}^{2}\cdots n_{b}^{2}}\int\limits_{0}^{1}x^{2n-1}\frac{(2\arccos(x))^{2a+1}}{(2a+1)!}dx

\displaystyle=\frac{\pi}{2}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n^{3}}\cdot\left(\sum\limits_{n_{1}<n_{2}<...<n_{b}<n}\frac{1}{n_{1}^{2}n_{2}^{2}...n_{b}^{2}}\right)\cdot\left(\sum\limits_{n<m_{1}<m_{2}<\cdots<m_{a}}\frac{1}{m_{1}^{2}m_{2}^{2}\cdots m_{a}^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\pi}{2}\sum\limits_{n_{1}<n_{2}<...<n_{b}<n<m_{1}<m_{2}<...<m_{a}}\frac{1}{n_{1}^{2}n_{2}^{2}...n_{b}^{2}n^{3}m_{1}^{2}m_{2}^{2}...m_{a}^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\pi}{2}H(a,b).\qed

6.2
Lifting Zagier’s formula

We will show in a sequence of lemmas that both sides of Zagier’s formula
H(a,b)=\hat{H}(a,b)
in the formal setting have the same image under the map
D_{<2a+2b+3}
. Thus by Theorem
5.54
their difference is a multiple of
\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(2a+2b+3)
. We determine this multiple
via the canonical map
\mathcal{Z}^{\mathsf{f}}\to\Z
.

Lemma 6.6
.

For integers
a,b\geq 0
and
1\leq r\leq a+b
we have

D_{2r+1}\big(\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(\{2\}^{a},3,\{2\}^{b})\big)=\pi_{2r+1}(\mathsf{a}(\xi_{a,b}^{r}))\otimes\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(\{2\}^{a+b+1-r})

where

\displaystyle\xi_{a,b}^{r}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq\alpha\leq a\\
0\leq\beta\leq b\\
\alpha+\beta+1=r\end{subarray}}\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(\{2\}^{\alpha},3,\{2\}^{\beta})-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq\alpha<a\\
0\leq\beta\leq b\\
\alpha+\beta+1=r\end{subarray}}\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(\{2\}^{\beta},3,\{2\}^{\alpha})

\displaystyle+2\,(\mathbb{I}(a\geq r)-\mathbb{I}(b\geq r))\sum_{i=1}^{r}(-1)^{i}\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(2i+1)\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(\{2\}^{r-i})

where
\mathbb{I}
denotes the indicator function (i. e.
\mathbb{I}(A)=1
if
A
is a true statement and
\mathbb{I}(A)=0
else).

Proof.

We have

\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(\{2\}^{a},3,\{2\}^{b})=\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}((x_{0}x_{1})^{a}x_{0}x_{0}x_{1}(x_{0}x_{1})^{b}).

By previous considerations, each summand in
D_{2r+1}(\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(\{2\}^{a},3,\{2\}^{b}))
corresponds to a consecutive subsequence of length
2r+3
(including boundaries). Let
\varepsilon_{0}\dots\varepsilon_{2r+2}
be such a subsequence.

Cases 1 + 2:
\varepsilon_{2r+2}
is to the left or
\varepsilon_{0}
is to the right of
x_{0}x_{0}
. Then, by parity,
\varepsilon_{0}=\varepsilon_{2r+2}
and the sequence does not contribute.
The general observation here is that the right tensor factor must be
\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(\{2\}^{a+b+1-r})
since
x_{0}x_{0}x_{1}
must be contained
\varepsilon_{0}\dots\varepsilon_{2r+2}
.

Cases 3 + 4: The sequence
x_{0}x_{0}x_{1}
is contained in
\varepsilon_{1}\dots\varepsilon_{2r+2}
.
In this case, we obtain factors of the form

\displaystyle\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(x_{1};(x_{0}x_{1})^{\alpha}x_{0}x_{0}x_{1}(x_{0}x_{1})^{\beta};x_{0})\vskip-8.5359pt

\displaystyle\alpha\leq a,\beta\leq b

\displaystyle\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(x_{0};(x_{1}x_{0})^{\alpha}x_{1}x_{0}x_{0}(x_{1}x_{0})^{\beta};x_{1})

\displaystyle\alpha<a,\beta\leq b

such that
\alpha+\beta+1=r
and
\alpha,\beta\geq 0
. Observe that these factors are
\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(\{2\}^{\alpha},3,\{2\}^{\beta})
and
-\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(\{2\}^{\beta},3,\{2\}^{\alpha})
, respectively.

Cases 5 + 6:
Assume that
\varepsilon_{0}\dots\varepsilon_{2r+2}
starts or ends in
x_{0}x_{0}
. This is only possible, if
b\geq r
or
a\geq r
. The corresponding factors are
\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(x_{1};w;x_{0})
and
\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(x_{0};w;x_{1})
where
w=(x_{0}x_{1})^{r}x_{0}
is palindromic. Hence
\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(x_{0};w;x_{1})=-\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(x_{1};w;x_{0})
. The claim now follows from Proposition
5.22
, i. e.

\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}((x_{0}x_{1})^{r}x_{0})=2\sum_{i=1}^{r}(-1)^{i}\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(2i+1)\,\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(\{2\}^{r-i}).\qed

Lemma 6.7
.

Assume the Kernel conjecture
5.52
, and let
a,b\geq 0
be given integers. Then there exist unique coefficients
\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{n}\in\mathbb{Q}
such that

\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(\{2\}^{a},3,\{2\}^{b})=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\alpha_{i}\,\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(2i+1)\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(\{2\}^{n-i})

with
n=a+b+1
.

Proof.

Let
w=(x_{0}x_{1})^{a}x_{0}x_{0}x_{1}(x_{0}x_{1})^{b}
.
We proof the statement by induction on
\operatorname{wt}(w)
. The claim is trivial for
\operatorname{wt}(w)=3
.
Let
w=\{2\}^{a}3\{2\}^{b}
with
\operatorname{wt}(w)=2N+1
and assume the claim holds for all
r<N
. Now Lemma
6.6
implies for all
1\leq r<N

D_{2r+1}(\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(w))=\pi_{2r+1}(\mathsf{a}(\xi_{a,b}^{r}))\otimes\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(\{2\}^{N-r}).

Recall also from Lemma
6.6
the explicit formula for
\xi_{a,b}^{r}
and observe that
\operatorname{wt}(\xi_{a,b}^{r})\leq\operatorname{wt}(w)-2
for all
r\leq a+b
.
So we can apply the induction hypothesis to all summands in
\xi_{a,b}^{r}
. Therefore,
\pi_{2r+1}(\mathsf{a}(\xi_{a,b}^{r}))=\alpha_{r}\,\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}_{2r+1}
.
Hence

D_{2r+1}(\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(w))=\alpha_{r}\,\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}_{2r+1}\otimes\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(\{2\}^{N-r}).

In particular, we obtain numbers
\alpha_{r}\in\mathbb{Q}
for each
1\leq r\leq N-1
such that

D_{<2N+1}(\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(w))=\sum_{r=1}^{N-1}\alpha_{r}\,\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}_{2r+1}\otimes\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(\{2\}^{N-r}).

On the other hand, we deduce from (
53
) that

D_{2r+1}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{N-1}\alpha_{i}\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(2i+1)\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(\{2\}^{N-i})\Big)=\alpha_{r}\,\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}_{2r+1}\otimes\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(\{2\}^{N-r}),

because
D_{2r+1}
is a derivation. Therefore,

D_{<2N+1}\Big(\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(w)-\sum_{i=1}^{N-1}\alpha_{i}\,\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(2i+1)\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(\{2\}^{N-i})\Big)=0.

By the assumption
\ker(D_{<2N+1})\cap\mathcal{Z}^{\mathsf{f}}_{2N+1}=\mathbb{Q}\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(2N+1)
it follows that both sides of the claim differ by
\alpha_{n}\,\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(2N+1)
for some
\alpha_{n}\in\mathbb{Q}
and the claim follows.
∎

Remark 6.8
.

Note that the linear combination

\Phi(\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(w))=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\alpha_{i}\,s_{2i+1}s_{2}^{n-i}

is unique where
\Phi\colon\mathcal{Z}^{\mathsf{f}}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{f}}
is the conjectured isomorphism from (
50
).
This may give an alternative proof.

Recall the numbers

c_{a,b}^{r}=2\cdot(-1)^{r}\Bigg(\binom{2r}{2b+2}-(1-2^{-2r})\binom{2r}{2a+1}\Bigg)

from Definition
4.30
that we studied in Section
4.3
.

Theorem 6.9
.

Assume the Kernel conjecture
5.52
, and let
a,b\geq 0
be given integers. Then we have

\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(\{2\}^{a},3,\{2\}^{b})=\sum_{r=1}^{a+b+1}c_{a,b}^{r}\,\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(2r+1)\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(\{2\}^{a+b+1-r}).

(62)

Proof.

Induction on the weight. Let
w=(x_{0}x_{1})^{a}x_{0}x_{0}x_{1}(x_{0}x_{1})^{b}
and
N=a+b
. By Lemma
6.7
we have

\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(\{2\}^{\alpha},3,\{2\}^{\beta})=\sum_{r=1}^{\alpha+\beta+1}\alpha_{r}\,\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(2r+1)\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(\{2\}^{\alpha+\beta+1-r})

We assume that
\alpha_{r}=c_{\alpha,\beta}^{r}

holds for all
\alpha,\beta\geq 0
such that
\alpha+\beta<N
. By Lemma
6.6
we have

D_{2r+1}(\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(w))=\pi_{2r+1}(\mathsf{a}(\xi_{a,b}^{r}))\otimes\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(\{2\}^{a+b+1-r})

for all
1\leq r\leq N
.
Applying the induction hypothesis to the explicit formula for
\xi_{a,b}^{r}
given in Lemma
6.6
yields modulo products

\pi_{2r+1}(\mathsf{a}(\xi_{a,b}^{r}))=\Big(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq\alpha\leq a\\
0\leq\beta\leq b\\
\alpha+\beta+1=r\end{subarray}}c_{\alpha,\beta}^{r}-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq\alpha<a\\
0\leq\beta\leq b\\
\alpha+\beta+1=r\end{subarray}}c_{\beta,\alpha}^{r}+2\cdot(-1)^{r}\big(\mathbb{I}(a\geq r)-\mathbb{I}(b\geq r)\big)\Big)\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}_{2r+1}.

By Lemma
4.33
we have

c_{a,b}^{r}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq\alpha\leq a\\
0\leq\beta\leq b\\
\alpha+\beta+1=r\end{subarray}}c_{\alpha,\beta}^{r}-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq\alpha<a\\
0\leq\beta\leq b\\
\alpha+\beta+1=r\end{subarray}}c_{\beta,\alpha}^{r}+2\cdot(-1)^{r}\big(\mathbb{I}(a\geq r)-\mathbb{I}(b\geq r)\big).

(63)

Hence for all
1\leq r\leq N
we have

D_{2r+1}\Big(\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(\{2\}^{a},3,\{2\}^{b})\Big)=c_{a,b}^{r}\,\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}_{2r+1}\otimes\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(\{2\}^{a+b+1-r}).

This implies with formula (
53
) applied to the products on the right hand side of the claimed identity (
62
) that

D_{<2N+3}\Big(\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(\{2\}^{a},3,\{2\}^{b})-\sum_{r=1}^{a+b+1}c_{a,b}^{r}\,\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(2r+1)\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(\{2\}^{a+b+1-r})\Big)=0.

By assumption, there is some
\alpha\in\mathbb{Q}
such that

\alpha\,\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(2N+3)=\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(\{2\}^{a},3,\{2\}^{b})-\sum_{r=1}^{a+b+1}c_{a,b}^{r}\,\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(2r+1)\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(\{2\}^{a+b+1-r}).

(64)

Applying the surjective morphism
\mathcal{Z}^{\mathsf{f}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{Z}
from (
42
) to (
64
) yields
\alpha=0
, because we have Zagier’s theorem.
∎

Applying the projection modulo products to (
62
), we deduce the following for the sets
\mathcal{B}^{1}
and
\mathcal{B}_{2,3}
from Definition
4.8
and
4.1
.

Corollary 6.10
.

Assuming the Kernel conjecture
5.52
, we have

\pi_{2r+1}\big(\mathsf{a}\big(\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(\mathcal{B}^{1})\big)\big)=\mathbb{Q}\,\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}_{2r+1},

and in particular

\pi_{2r+1}\big(\mathsf{a}\big(\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(\operatorname{gr}_{1}^{F}(\mathcal{B}_{2,3}))\big)\big)=\mathbb{Q}\,\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}_{2r+1}.

6.3
The final step in Brown’s proof

Definition 6.11
.

We set

\mathcal{Z}^{\mathsf{f}}_{2,3}=\operatorname{span}_{\mathbb{Q}}\{\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(k_{1},\dots,k_{d})\mid d\geq 0,k_{i}\in\{2,3\}\}.

On our way to prove that the free odd generation conjecture  implies
\mathcal{Z}^{\mathsf{f}}_{2,3}=\mathcal{Z}^{\mathsf{f}}
the next
ingredient is a detailed study of the coaction on
\mathcal{Z}^{\mathsf{f}}_{2,3}
.
We have
\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(\mathcal{B}_{2,3})=\mathcal{Z}^{\mathsf{f}}_{2,3}
and thus
by Lemma
4.3

\Delta_{\operatorname{Gon}}
restricts to

\Delta_{\operatorname{Gon}}\colon\mathcal{Z}^{\mathsf{f}}_{2,3}\rightarrow\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{f}}\otimes\mathcal{Z}^{\mathsf{f}}_{2,3},

According to Definition
4.1
, we are interested in the following filtration.

Definition 6.12
.

We define a
level filtration
on
\mathcal{Z}^{\mathsf{f}}_{2,3}
by setting

F_{\ell}\mathcal{Z}^{\mathsf{f}}_{2,3}=\operatorname{span}_{\mathbb{Q}}\Big\{\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(k_{1},\dots,k_{d})\in\mathcal{Z}^{\mathsf{f}}_{2,3}\;\Big|\;\deg_{3}(x_{0}^{k_{1}-1}x_{1}\cdots x_{0}^{k_{d}-1}x_{1})\leq\ell\Big\}

for all
\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
.

As a corollary of Lemma
4.3
we get

\Delta_{\operatorname{Gon}}\colon F_{\ell}\mathcal{Z}^{\mathsf{f}}_{2,3}\rightarrow\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{f}}\otimes F_{\ell}\mathcal{Z}^{\mathsf{f}}_{2,3}.

and Proposition
4.12
1. implies

D_{2r+1}(F_{\ell}\mathcal{Z}^{\mathsf{f}}_{2,3})\subseteq\mathcal{L}^{\mathsf{f}}_{2r+1}\otimes F_{\ell-1}\mathcal{Z}^{\mathsf{f}}_{2,3}.

(65)

Again we follow Definition
4.7
, and introduce the following.

Definition 6.13
.

For all
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
we set

\operatorname{gr}_{\ell}^{F}(\mathcal{Z}^{\mathsf{f}}_{2,3})={\mathchoice{\raisebox{4.49501pt}{$\displaystyle{F_{\ell}\mathcal{Z}^{\mathsf{f}}_{2,3}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-4.49501pt}{$\displaystyle{F_{\ell-1}\mathcal{Z}^{\mathsf{f}}_{2,3}}$}}{\raisebox{4.24501pt}{$\textstyle{F_{\ell}\mathcal{Z}^{\mathsf{f}}_{2,3}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-4.24501pt}{$\textstyle{F_{\ell-1}\mathcal{Z}^{\mathsf{f}}_{2,3}}$}}{\raisebox{3.24501pt}{$\scriptstyle{F_{\ell}\mathcal{Z}^{\mathsf{f}}_{2,3}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.24501pt}{$\scriptstyle{F_{\ell-1}\mathcal{Z}^{\mathsf{f}}_{2,3}}$}}{\raisebox{2.45833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{F_{\ell}\mathcal{Z}^{\mathsf{f}}_{2,3}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.45833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{F_{\ell-1}\mathcal{Z}^{\mathsf{f}}_{2,3}}$}}}

and
\operatorname{gr}_{0}^{F}=F_{0}\mathcal{Z}^{\mathsf{f}}_{2,3}
. For all
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
we denote by
\pi_{\ell}^{F}\colon F_{\ell}\mathcal{Z}^{\mathsf{f}}_{2,3}\twoheadrightarrow\operatorname{gr}_{\ell}^{F}\mathcal{Z}^{\mathsf{f}}_{2,3}

the natural projections.

By (
65
) the map
D_{2r+1}^{(\ell)}=(\operatorname{id}\otimes\pi_{\ell-1}^{F})\circ D_{2r+1}\rvert_{F_{\ell}\mathcal{Z}^{\mathsf{f}}_{2,3}}
induces a map

D_{2r+1}^{(\ell)}\colon\operatorname{gr}_{\ell}^{F}\mathcal{Z}^{\mathsf{f}}_{2,3}\rightarrow\mathcal{L}^{\mathsf{f}}_{2r+1}\otimes\operatorname{gr}_{\ell-1}^{F}\mathcal{Z}^{\mathsf{f}}_{2,3}

(66)

for all
r,\ell\geq 1
. We have the following refinement of (
66
), that combines
Theorem
4.9
with the lift of Zagier’s formula (
62
).

Theorem 6.14
.

Assume the Kernel conjecture
5.52
holds.
Let
r,\ell\geq 1
. Then for each weight
N\geq 2r+1
, we have

D_{2r+1}^{(\ell)}\colon\operatorname{gr}_{\ell}^{F}(\mathcal{Z}^{\mathsf{f}}_{2,3})_{N}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}\,\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}_{2r+1}\otimes\operatorname{gr}_{\ell-1}^{F}(\mathcal{Z}^{\mathsf{f}}_{2,3})_{N-2r-1}.

Proof.

Recall the derivations

D_{2r+1}^{(\ell)}\colon\operatorname{gr}_{\ell}^{F}(\mathcal{B}_{2,3})_{N}\rightarrow\pi_{2r+1}(\mathcal{B}^{1})\otimes\operatorname{gr}_{\ell-1}^{F}(\mathcal{B}_{2,3})_{N-2r-1}

from Proposition
4.12
.
As a consequence of lifting Zagier’s formula (
62
), we observed in Corollary
6.10
that
\pi_{2r+1}\big(\mathsf{a}\big(\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(\mathcal{B}^{1})\big)\big)=\mathbb{Q}\,\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}_{2r+1}
.
The claim now follows by applying the maps
\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}
and
\overline{\mathsf{a}\circ\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}}
to the dervations above since we have
\overline{\mathsf{a}\circ\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}}(\pi_{2r+1}(\mathcal{B}^{1}))\subseteq\pi_{2r+1}(\mathsf{a}(\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(\mathcal{B}^{1})))
.
∎

Assuming the free odd generation conjecture
5.44
, Proposition
5.50
states that
\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}_{2r+1}
is non-zero.
Therefore we get the identifications

\rho_{r}\colon\mathbb{Q}\,\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}_{2r+1}\otimes\operatorname{gr}_{\ell-1}^{F}(\mathcal{Z}^{\mathsf{f}}_{2,3})_{N}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{gr}_{\ell-1}^{F}(\mathcal{Z}^{\mathsf{f}}_{2,3})_{N}

induced by
\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}_{2r+1}\mapsto 1
for all
r,\ell,N\in\mathbb{N}
.

Definition 6.15
.

Assuming the free odd generation conjecture,  we define for all
N,\ell\geq 1
the maps

D_{<N}^{(\ell)}\colon\operatorname{gr}_{\ell}^{F}(\mathcal{Z}^{\mathsf{f}}_{2,3})_{N}\rightarrow\bigoplus_{3\leq 2r+1\leq N}\operatorname{gr}_{\ell-1}^{F}(\mathcal{Z}^{\mathsf{f}}_{2,3})_{N-2r-1}

via

D_{<N}^{(\ell)}=\bigoplus_{3\leq 2r+1\leq N}\rho_{r}\circ D_{2r+1}^{(\ell)}.

Observe that

\bigoplus_{3\leq 2r+1\leq N}\operatorname{gr}_{\ell-1}^{F}(\mathcal{Z}^{\mathsf{f}}_{2,3})_{N-2r-1}=\operatorname{gr}_{\ell-1}^{F}(\mathcal{Z}^{\mathsf{f}}_{2,3})_{<N-1}

since the weight gives a grading on
\mathcal{Z}^{\mathsf{f}}
.

Lemma 6.16
.

If free odd generation conjecture
5.44
holds, then
the diagram

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern (67)

is well-defined and commutative.

Proof.

Recall
\mathchoice{\mathop{}\kern 5.30833pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\partial}}^{\mathmakebox[0pt][l]{\mathmakebox[r]{\phi}}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.30833pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\partial}}^{\mathmakebox[0pt][l]{\mathmakebox[r]{\phi}}}}{\mathop{}\kern 6.67831pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\partial}}^{\mathmakebox[0pt][l]{\mathmakebox[r]{\phi}}}}{\mathop{}\kern 7.5657pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\partial}}^{\mathmakebox[0pt][l]{\mathmakebox[r]{\phi}}}}\partial^{(\ell)}_{<N}
is given in Definition
4.14
and by Theorem
4.27
, the upper row is an isomorphism. The lower horizontal map is given by Definition
6.15
and it is well-defined by the discussion before its definition. Since
\mathchoice{\mathop{}\kern 5.30833pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\partial}}^{\mathmakebox[0pt][l]{\mathmakebox[r]{\phi}}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.30833pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\partial}}^{\mathmakebox[0pt][l]{\mathmakebox[r]{\phi}}}}{\mathop{}\kern 6.67831pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\partial}}^{\mathmakebox[0pt][l]{\mathmakebox[r]{\phi}}}}{\mathop{}\kern 7.5657pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\partial}}^{\mathmakebox[0pt][l]{\mathmakebox[r]{\phi}}}}\partial^{(\ell)}_{<N}
and
D_{<N}^{(\ell)}
are compatible by Remark
4.5
, it suffices to show for
w\in\mathcal{B}^{1}
that

\phi(w)\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}_{2r+1}=\pi_{2r+1}(\mathsf{a}(\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(w))).

But now, observing that projecting the identities (
43
) and (
62
) modulo products and weight gives exactly the coefficients
\phi(w)
from Definition
4.13
.
∎

Remark 6.17
.

We may change the assumptions for
Lemma
6.16
a little bit, namely by imposing that the Kernel conjecture
5.52
holds and that
\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}_{2r+1}\neq 0\in\mathcal{L}^{\mathsf{f}}_{2r+1}
for all
r\in\mathbb{N}
.

Theorem 6.18
.

Assume the free odd generation conjecture
5.44
.
Then the elements

\{\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(k_{1},\ldots,k_{d})\mid k_{i}\in\{2,3\}\}

are linearly independent.

Proof.

Recall that diagram (
67
) commutes by Lemma
6.16
because of our assumption of the free odd generation conjecture.  By Theorem
4.27
, the upper row is an isomorphism and obviously the vertical maps are epimorphisms. We prove by induction on the level
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
that they are in fact isomorphisms.

We start with the case
\ell=1
and
N\in\mathbb{N}
arbitrary. Since
\mathcal{Z}^{\mathsf{f}}
is a weight-graded algebra, the elements
\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(\{2\}^{n})
are linearly independent for all
n\geq 0
. We deduce that the right-hand side of (
67
) is an isomorphism. Thus the left-hand side must also be an isomorphism.

Clearly, the same lines of argument would hold for all
\ell>1
, thus (
67
) is a commutative diagram of isomorphisms. Now, there are no relations in different weights and if there were a relation in different level, then it must induce a relation in the corresponding graded pieces. But we have shown that this is impossible, thus the claim follows.
∎

Theorem 6.19
.

Assume the free odd generation conjecture
5.44
, then

\mathcal{Z}^{\mathsf{f}}=\mathcal{Z}^{\mathsf{f}}_{2,3}.

Proof.

Under the assumption of the free odd generation conjecture,  Theorem
6.18
implies that
\{\zeta(k_{1},\ldots,k_{d})\mid k_{i}\in\{2,3\}\}
forms a basis of
\mathcal{Z}^{\mathsf{f}}_{2,3}
. Counting the number of such indices in a given weight
N
yields

\sum_{N\geq 0}\dim\big((\mathcal{Z}^{\mathsf{f}}_{2,3})_{N}\big)\,x^{N}=\frac{1}{1-x^{2}-x^{3}}.

This agrees with the computed dimensions for
\mathcal{Z}^{\mathsf{f}}
in Proposition
5.46
. We deduce that the two spaces
\mathcal{Z}^{\mathsf{f}}
and
\mathcal{Z}^{\mathsf{f}}_{2,3}
must agree.
∎

Remark 6.20
.

Finally we observe that Brown’s proof for the generators also works in the formal setup: Assume the free odd generation conjecture  holds, then
any element in
\mathcal{Z}^{\mathsf{f}}
can be written uniquely as a polynomial of
elements
\zeta^{\mathsf{f}}(w)
, where
w
is a Lyndon word in
\mathsf{2}
and
\mathsf{3}
.
